# exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

Independently audit finite Boolean disjoint substitution and fresh-selector shared-data closure, for every finite arity/depth allowed by their hypotheses. Entropy is in bits; squared coefficients use the normalized product Fourier basis. Write I_mu for spectral expected cardinality, V_mu=Var_mu, delta_mu=I_mu-V_mu. Under nonuniform inputs I_mu differs from the unweighted physical pivotal sum. For coordinate probability p_j, sigma_j^2=4p_j(1-p_j), and

    Hplus_mu(f)=sum_{S nonempty} fhat_mu(S)^2 log2(prod_{j in S} sigma_j^2/fhat_mu(S)^2).

This is corrected entropy, not ordinary Shannon entropy H_mu or conditional nonempty entropy. Uniform-cube Hplus=H-h0, h0=(Ef)^2 log2(1/(Ef)^2). Full FEI remains OPEN.

# complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

## Disjoint coefficient reconstruction, all child biases

Let independent product physical blocks B_i carry Boolean f_i with mean m_i and variance v_i=1-m_i^2>0. Their sign outputs have probabilities q_i=(1+m_i)/2 and are independent. Expand outer Boolean g under this induced product law nu in psi_i(z)=(z_i-m_i)/sqrt(v_i). The normalized centered child has expansion

    (f_i-m_i)/sqrt(v_i)=sum_{T nonempty in B_i} fhat_i(T) phi_T/sqrt(v_i).

For a physical Fourier set S, its occupied block set A={i:S intersect B_i is nonempty} and the individual T_i=S intersect B_i are uniquely determined. Consequently

    Fhat_mu(S)=ghat_nu(A) prod_{i in A} fhat_i(T_i)/sqrt(v_i),
    Fhat_mu(empty)=ghat_nu(empty),       F=g(f_1,...,f_k).

There is one decomposition, so no coherent cross term is discarded. Sample A from ghat_nu^2; for each i in A sample a nonempty T_i from fhat_i^2/v_i; output their union. This experiment is bijective on its positive outcomes. Set w_i=I_i^nu(g), a_i=w_i/v_i. Then

    V_mu(F)=V_nu(g),
    I_mu(F)=sum_i a_i I_mu_i(f_i),
    H_mu(F)=H_nu(g)+sum_i a_i[H_mu_i(f_i)-h2(v_i)],
    Hplus_mu(F)=Hplus_nu(g)+sum_i a_i Hplus_mu_i(f_i),
    delta_mu(F)=delta_nu(g)+sum_i a_i delta_mu_i(f_i).

For full entropy the conditional child entropy is [H(f_i)-h2(v_i)]/v_i. For corrected entropy the log numerator splits into an outer product of v_i and the physical sigma factors. This is exactly the needed cancellation. Since all a_i and deficits are nonnegative, gate/child inequalities Hplus<=C_i delta imply Hplus(F)<=max_i C_i delta(F), including the outer constant C_0.

For balanced children v_i=1 and nu is uniform, hence H(F)=H(g)+sum_i Inf_i(g)H(f_i). Ordinary FEI estimates H<=CI alone do not cancel the outer entropy. Strong deficit estimates are sufficient. For unbalanced children, retaining the old uniform outer spectrum is unsupported.

Boundary cases are exact. If v_i=0, freeze that child output and delete its gate input BEFORE division; at product-bias endpoints delete deterministic physical inputs first. Functions differing only on zero-probability assignments are identified after that restriction. If all children are constant, F is constant and H=I=delta=0. If outer input i is irrelevant, w_i=0 and its child makes no contribution. For interior nonconstant children a_i=E[(D_i g)^2], where D_i is the sign derivative on other outputs, so 0<=a_i<=1: a tiny v_i introduces no intrinsic blowup of this physical multiplier. A biased literal has Hplus=delta=0 although ordinary spectral H=h2(v_i) has a divergent H/I endpoint ratio. A uniform balanced Boolean of I=1 is a signed dictator (spectral support only on singletons and Booleanity forces a single coefficient), so its entropy is zero; division by I-1 at this terminal is forbidden.

At any physical restriction cylinder, unrevealed inputs are still independent product inputs and blocks remain disjoint. Recompute m_i, v_i, the reduced gate and its induced nu. For one changed child, write g=A+z_i B before restriction. If its new mean is m, the new outer polynomial is A+mB+sqrt(1-m^2)psi_i B; coefficients not containing i are Ahat(T)+m Bhat(T), whose squared values contain 2m Ahat(T)Bhat(T). At m=+/-1 freeze the gate input. Conditioning on an OUTPUT event is not a physical cylinder and does not justify the same product premise. This reconstructs A056's principal claims without borrowing its implementation.

## Gate admission and prime reductions

The exact identity admits symmetric threshold gates under every induced product bias with C_T=80+460/ln2, and parity with 2/ln2, using the cited gate lemmas. Uniform regular gates have a strong bound 4C_reg; an arbitrary weighted LTF internal gate is admissible by that uniform theorem only when all remaining nonconstant children are balanced. Uniform-input LTF terminal blocks can be unbalanced outputs: their bound Hplus<=4C_reg delta is a physical-input statement, and the next gate must then accept those biases. Negations preserve corrected entropy/deficit when the corresponding input law is flipped.

Fixed finite gate bases may use their separately established all-bias constants. A prime decomposition gives a conditional reduction only if every prime gate in it has a valid bound at every induced bias and one constant uniform over unbounded prime arity. The identities neither establish that prime estimate nor justify overlap of physical child blocks. The dyadic gate-potential formulation in biased.tex is FEI-equivalent, not a solution: finite uniform encoders realize only dyadic q; extension of the infimum-defined potential to nondyadic q is an additional unproved step. For arbitrary product inputs, actual physical probabilities and the admitted gate inequalities must instead cover their real induced q directly.

## Shared-data collision reconstruction and constant preservation

Let x be a fresh uniform a-bit selector independent of a common uniform data cube y, N=2^a, with Boolean branches g_b(y), including biased or constant branches. Define

    c_b(T)=ghat_b(T), R_T=N^-1 sum_b c_b(T)^2,
    r_T(b)=c_b(T)^2/(N R_T),
    nu_T(S)=[N^-1 sum_b chi_S(b)c_b(T)]^2/R_T  (R_T>0).

The actual physical Fourier coefficient is Fhat(S,T)=N^-1 sum_b chi_S(b)c_b(T), retaining its sign. Parseval over x gives sum_S Fhat(S,T)^2=R_T, including T=empty. A comparison law P(B=b,T)=c_b(T)^2/N has entropy a+average_b H(g_b), marginal R and conditional B law r_T. The actual law has the same R and conditional selector law nu_T. Subtraction of the two chain rules proves

    H(F)=a+average_b H(g_b)+sum_T R_T[H(nu_T)-H(r_T)],
    H(F)<=2a+average_b H(g_b).

Physical conditioning proves I_data(F)=average_b I(g_b), and

    Inf_{x_j}(F)=N^-1 sum_b P_y[g_b!=g_{b^j}]
               =N^-1 sum_b (1-E_y[g_b g_{b^j}])/2.

If every neighboring correlation is zero, I_sel=a/2. If instead I_sel>=c a for a fixed c>0, 2a<=2 I_sel/c. Combining branch ordinary FEI H(g_b)<=C I(g_b) proves respectively max{4,C} and max{2/c,C}. This is existing baseline closure, not new research. The weaker sufficient hypothesis is the actual averaged energy; pairwise neighboring orthogonality is one way to supply it. Nonneighbor branches need not be orthogonal. Spectral supports may overlap: only the old partition-selector mixture identity requires their disjointness.

For recursion, apply this local result at each node on the union of its branch data coordinates. Irrelevant padding changes no branch quantity. Internal branches may reuse each other's data, including selectors introduced LOWER in the construction. The CURRENT node's selector coordinates must be absent from ALL its branches, and independent under the actual input law. Induction from regular leaves preserves C_reg>4 without accumulating depth or a worst-case data-read count. If a=0 the function is the one branch. At zero selector energy all branches agree on every neighboring pair, hence all are the same Boolean function and F is that branch; the loose 2a bound should be bypassed. Constants are permitted in the shared-data identity, but two constant neighboring branches cannot have zero correlation. A constant branch may be orthogonal to a balanced one.

Reusing the current selector invalidates the independence premise: g_+(x)=x and g_-(x)=-x produce F(x)=1 when selected by that same x. Treating the branch x as independent data instead produces xy, with I=2, a different physical function. Freshness cannot be inferred from a drawn tree alone.

## Explicit controls and first unsupported lines

Old disjoint-support partition-selector formulas fail for leaves y and -y: actual F=xy has H=0,I=2, whereas the label mixture predicts H=2,I=3/2. Conversely orthogonal shared branches may have common Fourier indices with signed cancellation, as the paper's majority pair shows. Arbitrary signed singleton leaves g_u(y)=s_u y give F=s_x y and encode arbitrary Boolean s, with H(F)=H(s), I(F)=I(s)+1. Thus declaring arbitrary shared-data leaves an easy terminal assumes an FEI-strength conclusion.

The bent-splice control is independently replayed at odd m=3,5,7. Its two Boolean branches have uniform spectra on the even and odd subset classes: H=m-1,I=m/2, and local spectral erasure zero. The fresh one-bit splice has flat spectrum on all m+1 bits: H=m+1,I=(m+1)/2, selector influence 1/2. Hence zero child erasure is not a terminal entropy certificate. Here total data influence remains large, so this does not refute ordinary max{4,C} closure.

All three weighted controls come from an actual increasing threshold event

    A={m X0+sum_{j=1}^m Xj>=m},
    q=P(Xj=1), a=q^m,
    b=P(X0=1)=(1-eta)a/[eta+(1-eta)a],
    alpha=a/[eta+(1-eta)a].

Conditionally on A, X0=0 with probability eta and all light bits are one; otherwise they are independent Bernoulli q. Thus, with natural binary entropy h,

    TC(Q)=m[h(eta+(1-eta)q)-(1-eta)h(q)],
    I_mu/(4alpha)=1-eta-b+m eta(1-q),
    rho0=1-a, rho_light=(1-b)q^(m-1).

For q=m^-2, eta=1/m, d=1+(m-1)a, alpha=ma/d, one has TC=ln m+1+O(ln m/m) and I/(4alpha)=2-1/m-q-b. This proves alpha TC/I=(ln m+1)/8+O(ln m/m). For V=4alpha(1-alpha), the credit arguments are (1-a)/[alpha(1-alpha)] and m/(1-alpha), both >1. Put L=ln(1/alpha)=2m ln m-ln m+ln d. The residual divided by 4alpha is exactly

    (1-alpha)[L-ln4-ln(1-alpha)]
    -(1-1/m-b)[L+ln(1-a)-ln(1-alpha)]
    -(1-q)[ln m-ln(1-alpha)].

As a=m^(-2m), terms of size alpha L and bL vanish; the expression equals ln m-ln4+O(ln m/m). Division by I/(4alpha) gives the second no-go, .5 ln m-ln2+O(ln m/m). It refutes coefficient ONE on the credits, not every larger coefficient. Corrected Hplus/delta still tends to 2: for the light AND child delta=4a[m(1-q)-1+a] and Hplus=2delta+8ma(q-a)log2(1/q); the outer two-input OR has both deficit and corrected entropy O(ma^2), and light multiplier 1-b. These are decomposable weighted thresholds with divergent proposed PHYSICAL TC payments and a harmless actual spectral ratio.

For eta=1/2 at fixed m and q->0, Q tends to the equal mixture of (1,0,...,0) and (0,1,...,1). Every pairwise physical mutual information tends to ln2, and I_j/alpha->2 for every j. Therefore alpha sum_{k!=j} I_Q(Xj;Xk)/I_j -> m ln2/2 simultaneously for all j. This refutes even existential payment of that physical conditional-TC recursion. It is not the spectral quantity M_A=H(S_A|S_B).

B09 Section 5: tensor additivity of M_A,J_A,Delta_A is correct for disjoint PRODUCTS f=prod_r f_r with aligned physical blocks. Spectra and physical-child/spectral experiments then factor, so chain rules add. It does not extend unchanged to arbitrary disjoint outer substitution. A simple counter to child-ledger-only additivity is sign AND of two fresh uniform literals. Standalone literals have M=J=0 under any one-bit/full or empty reveal. The composed sign AND has four spectral masses 1/4, M_{first bit}=1 and J_{first bit}=1: both restricted children have entropy zero and the residual spectral bit is fair. An outer residual must be accounted for. The precise unsupported sentence is B09 Section 5's inference from tensor orthogonality of the transform to additivity of all aligned ledgers for broader gate composition. This audit does not assert a counterexample to a separately specified weighted cocycle; no such general M,J cocycle was proved there.

The B09 core uncertainty upper bound is not refuted by this scope objection. Its Section 5(4) inference that EVERY O(p) tax is impossible does not follow from the displayed upper-bound tax asymptotic alone; a necessary-tax lower-bound family is required. No endpoint sharpness acceptance is issued here. Prime-gate/low-energy terminal FEI beyond the admitted families remains unproved at the first missing all-bias gate payment or signed collision payment, respectively.

# exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

    L_A=M_A+J_A,

# complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

## Actual physical-child probability law

Use phi_i(x_i)=(x_i-p_i)/sqrt(p_i(1-p_i)) on {0,1}. Write c_{R,T}=fhat_p(R union T), R subset A, T subset B, and q_T=sum_R c_{R,T}^2. Expanding on A and using product Parseval gives

    fhat_{z,p_B}(T)=sum_R c_{R,T} phi_R(z),
    E_z fhat_z(T)^2=q_T.

Define the joint law P(Z=z,T)=P_A(z) fhat_z(T)^2. Booleanity is essential for its physical marginal: every child has squared norm one, so summing over T gives P(Z=z)=P_A(z). Its T marginal is q. Therefore

    J_A=H(q)-E_z H(fhat_z^2)=I(Z;T)>=0,
    L_A=H(fhat_p^2)-E_z H(fhat_z^2)=M_A+J_A.

Zero-q fibers are omitted; all other spectra are probability laws. For A empty the experiment is unchanged and L=M=J=Delta=0; for B empty T is deterministic, J=0 and M=L=H(fhat_p^2). A normalized real function with nonconstant child squared norms does not have the required physical marginal: for example sqrt(2)1_{x=1} at fair x has child norms zero and two. Normalizing its children independently changes the experiment. This is why a unit vector alone is not a substitute for the Boolean physical source.

## Primary uncertainty application

For q_T>0, normalize a_R=c_{R,T}/sqrt(q_T) and b_z=sqrt(P_A(z)) fhat_z(T)/sqrt(q_T). Both vectors have norm one, and b=U_A a, where

    U_A=tensor_{i in A} [[sqrt(1-p_i),-sqrt(p_i)],
                         [sqrt(p_i),sqrt(1-p_i)]].

Its maximum entry magnitude is c_A=prod_i sqrt(max(p_i,1-p_i)); both bases are orthonormal. The primary Shannon theorem yields H(a^2)+H(b^2)>=-2log2 c_A. Averaging in q gives M_A+H(Z|T)>=-sum_i log2 max(p_i,1-p_i). Since H(Z)=sum_i h2(p_i), subtraction proves J_A<=M_A+Delta_A. Together with the previous identity and J_A>=0, this proves the core for arbitrary finite blocks, not just singletons.

No Boolean constraint on the individual normalized spectral fiber is required by that theorem. Its use is valid for real amplitudes because they are a special case of complex vectors. Global Booleanity was used separately to identify the physical marginal. An empty block is treated directly rather than invoking the article's nontrivial-overlap wording.

## Exact bias endpoints and tax normalization

Put r=min(p,1-p). The one-coordinate tax is exactly

    Delta(p)=r log2((1-r)/r),       0<r<=1/2.

It is nonnegative, symmetric under p<->1-p, zero at p=1/2 and tends to zero at both deterministic endpoints. More precisely,

    Delta(p)-r log2(1/r)=r log2(1-r),
    -r^2/[(1-r)ln2] <= remainder <= -r^2/ln2.

The inequalities follow by integrating 1/(1-u) on [0,r]. Thus the available tax has leading coefficient one and a negative second-order correction. This is an upper-bound tax, not a necessity/optimality result; the newer sharp scalar tax is a separate claim. Deterministic coordinates are deleted before constructing a normalized basis, so no p=0 or p=1 division occurs. On a fixed finite truth table, coefficients whose sets contain a degenerating coordinate have vanishing squared mass, and all other masses converge to those of the supported restriction; finite Shannon entropy is continuous. The limiting inequality therefore agrees with coordinate contraction. The two endpoints cannot be handled by pretending the singular basis is already defined there.

## Corrected entropy and deficit transport into C019

Fix the original physical product probabilities, and let a node reveal a block A. Write sigma_i^2=4p_i(1-p_i), h0(f)=P_f(empty)log2(1/P_f(empty)), and

    Eplus_p(f)=H_p(f)-h0(f)+sum_i I_i^p(f)log2 sigma_i^2,
    delta_p(f)=I_p(f)-V_p(f).

Unrevealed-coordinate spectral influences average exactly to their parent values, by the q marginal identity. Let

    I_A=sum_S |S intersect A| P_f(S),
    K_A=sum_{nonempty S subset A} P_f(S).

Then I_parent-E I_child=I_A and V_parent-E V_child=K_A. Hence the deficit spent at this physical node is

    d_A=I_A-K_A>=0.

The inequality follows termwise: a nonempty set contained in A spends |S|-1; a set meeting B spends |S intersect A|. In particular a singleton reveal spends I_i-P_f({i}), not the entire I_i deficit budget.

The exact corrected-entropy decrement is

    Eplus_parent-E Eplus_child
      = M_A+J_A+sum_{i in A} I_i log2 sigma_i^2
        +E h0(child)-h0(parent).                         (1)

Now take any finite adaptive physical restriction tree. Each internal node's block is chosen from the already observed physical history; the leaf partition consists of product-law cylinders. Its probability w_v is the product of actual physical branch probabilities, not an artificial fair weight. At each node use (1), then sum with w_v. Every h0 term cancels except root and leaves. The resulting identities are

    Eplus_root=E Eplus_leaf
       +sum_v w_v[M_v+J_v+sum_{i in A_v}I_i(f_v)log2 sigma_i^2]
       +E h0(leaf)-h0(root),                             (2)
    delta_root=E delta_leaf+sum_v w_v d_v.               (3)

Substitute only the accepted core to obtain the valid sufficient upper ledger

    Eplus_root <= E Eplus_leaf
       +sum_v w_v[2M_v+Delta_v+sum_{i in A_v}I_i(f_v)log2 sigma_i^2]
       +E h0(leaf)-h0(root).                             (4)

For terminal inequalities Eplus_leaf<=C delta_leaf, the exact unproved condition for a C-deficit payment is that the complete nonterminal term in (2), including its terminal h0 reserve, is <=C sum_v w_v d_v. The stronger sufficient condition replaces M+J by 2M+Delta as in (4). These are explicit obligations, not established bounds. At uniform original inputs the sigma logs vanish and Delta=0; converting to full H gives the usual C019 terminal-inclusive entropy/influence telescope. The biased core is thus a valid dependency, but pays no unpaid M or terminal entropy by itself.

A biased literal demonstrates the required cancellation. For f=2x-1, v=4p(1-p), its spectrum is (1-v,v), Eplus=delta=0. A full physical reveal has M=h2(v), J=0 and I_A=v. Exactly

    h2(v)+v log2(v)-h0(f)=0.

Removing the negative sigma log or empty-atom reserve would turn a certified zero-deficit terminal into spurious rare-bias debt. Even if those terms are kept, the crude MU sufficient ledger pays an excess h2(v)+Delta(p)>0 at this zero-deficit node; one must stop at the already certified literal terminal or use the exact decrement. This illustrates insufficiency of the upper ledger, not failure of the core or corrected FEI. Likewise irrelevant inputs contribute no actual decrement but a positive raw coordinate tax at nonuniform bias; contracting them is necessary before treating tax as a payment budget.

**First unsupported universal line:** the expectation of the entire corrected nonterminal charge in (2), with terminal h0 reserve and no reuse of d_v, is bounded by an absolute multiple of the root deficit spent. Equivalently, in the original uniform formulation one still needs paid conditional spectral erasure plus certified terminal entropy. No gate primality, scalar sharpness or finite table check establishes that line.

# complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

REVIEW_A059.md independently reconstructs the complete claim. At controller steps U is a martingale and

    kappa=R-E R_child=sum_j q_j V_j D_2(P(X|C_j)||fair)>=0.

At a data query in state j, q_j remains fixed, V_j-E V_j,child=beta_j^2=t, and the exact signed empty-core split is

    D_iH(F)=U-E U_child+M(t)+N-q_j eta(t).

Other-row Parseval yields masses 1-q_j V_j and q_j V_j. The positive cap therefore obeys cap<=eta(q_j)t+q_j t/ln2+q_j eta(t). Its singleton entropy term cancels the exact negative q_j eta(t), and N<=2q_j t/ln2 gives the signed DATA bound with coefficient 3/ln2. This retains the mass correction at biased projected rows; no equal-mass JS is substituted for the raw gap.

Finite telescoping on the same actual tree proves

    R_root=E R_leaf+sum_data w eta(q_j)beta_j^2+sum_control w kappa,
    I_root=Pi_data+Pi_control+E I_leaf,
    H_root<=E H_leaf+U_root-E U_leaf+R_root-E R_leaf
                                +(3/ln2)T_data+sum_control w(D-kappa),
    sum_data w cap+E R_leaf+sum_control w kappa
                                <=[(1+2/e)/ln2]Pi_data+R_root.

Indicator isoperimetry and independent-core mismatch on the actual control edges give initial R_root<=2I_ctrl(root). That initial account is not reopened at each depth and is not an extra free controller entropy payment. The source's dependency alias A056-BIASED-ERASURE@2 identifies the recentered interface exported as A056-DISJOINT-COMPOSITION@2; the used algebra is independently reconstructed here. B20 should reconcile the alias before index adoption. This naming issue requires no mathematical repair.

POINT_CONTROL_PAYMENT.md derives the next step, rather than simply asserting a complete activation bound. At any live point-control node let q=2^-s, s>=1, m be the actual remaining core mean, a=1-m, V=1-m^2 and z=qa. The physical queried influence is z and kappa=qV. Separating nonempty data rows, empty-data nonempty-control rows and the fully empty row gives exactly

    B_control-kappa=(q-2q^2)a^2+Phi((1-z)^2,z^2),
    Phi(A,B)=(A+B)h2(B/(A+B)).

This is nonnegative. Since a<=2 it is <=2z(1-z)+Phi((1-z)^2,z^2)<=3z. The last scalar estimate is the existing A019 packet bound, independently reconstructed with amplitude entropy and a small-z logarithmic estimate; it is not a new scalar optimization. The full physical child-channel gap satisfies 0<=J<=2z/ln2 by the closed two-point scalar inequality reconstructed in the same note. Consequently every controller step obeys the new price D-kappa<=(3+2/ln2)z, at every biased or constant descendant. Negative constant cores have V=kappa=0 but their nonzero point-controller entropy is still charged. Positive constant cores and failed activations give zero cost.

Summing under actual reaches, for arbitrary stopping and interleaving, proves the scoped terminal-inclusive continuation

    H_root<=U_root+R_root+(3/ln2)T_data
                     +(3+2/ln2)Pi_control+E[H_leaf-U_leaf-R_leaf].

The original U_root=q E_ne(u) and actual terminal functional are retained. For every exclusive independent-core controller, let g(z)=sum m_j C_j(z) and gamma_j(T)=Chat_j(T)^2/q_j. The disjoint nonempty core frequencies give the exact factorization

    H(F)-U-R=Eraw(ghat^2)+sum_j q_j V_j H(gamma_j)>=0.

Eraw uses raw squared amplitudes, not a normalized mean-controller spectrum. At an ACTUAL controller-resolved leaf gamma_j is an empty point mass, so this terminal quantity equals eta(m_j^2)<=Var(u_j)/ln2<=I(F_leaf)/ln2. Thus the displayed point-activation stopping inequality has a separately priced remaining-influence terminal whenever its controller state is resolved. An unresolved controller with constant cores can still retain its entire arbitrary spectral entropy. Replacing actual frontiers, importing output entropy, or spending the same remaining influence twice would not establish this boundary result.

First unsupported universal line: pay inherited U_root and unresolved terminal H-U-R plus general exclusive-controller residual from one unspent influence/deficit account under an admissible physical policy. Constant-state arbitrary controllers have U=R=kappa=0, so a universal residual price cannot be inferred from this point geometry. Shared physical data also violates the reviewed independent-core decomposition. The new point debit and controller-resolved boundary supply their stated interfaces; they supply no universal terminal/core admission.

# complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

Reconstruct f from the four physical values (1,1,1,-1), in little-endian order x+2y. Since f=1-2XY, its centered unnormalized expansion is obtained by expanding X=p+(X-p), Y=q+(Y-q). The coefficients are 1-2pq, -2q, -2p and -2 in the basis 1, X-p, Y-q, (X-p)(Y-q). Multiplying squared coefficients by the corresponding basis variances yields

    a=(1-2pq)^2,
    b=4p(1-p)q^2,
    c=4p^2q(1-q),
    d=4p(1-p)q(1-q).

These are nonnegative and sum to one. They have been independently obtained by a recursive pair transform on the original values; no supplied spectrum was taken as an input. Write u=4q(1-q), r=pu. Then c+d=r. The X=0 restriction is constant; the X=1 restriction is 1-2Y with spectral law (1-u,u). The physical joint channel is therefore

    P(X=0,S_Y=0)=1-p,
    P(X=1,S_Y=0)=p(1-u),
    P(X=1,S_Y=1)=pu,
    P(X=0,S_Y=1)=0.

An independent conditional-law calculation gives

    M=(1-r)h2(b/(a+b))+r h2(d/(c+d)),
    J=h2(p)-(1-r)h2(p(1-u)/(1-r)).

The entropy chain rule equivalently gives M=H(a,b,c,d)-h2(r) and J=h2(r)-p h2(u). The checker encloses both implementations and checks their overlap. Hence E=2h2(pu)-H(a,b,c,d)-p h2(u).

For fixed positive alpha and t=alpha p+O(p^2), -t log2(t)=alpha p log2(1/p)+O_alpha(p). To justify the remainder: t/(alpha p)=1+O(p) has logarithm O(p), and p^2 log2(1/p)=O(p). Mass 1-O(p) contributes O(p), because -(1-s)ln(1-s)<=s; a mass O(p^2) contributes O(p^2 log(1/p)). With fixed interior q, b=4q^2p+O_q(p^2), d=4q(1-q)p+O_q(p^2), c=O_q(p^2). Thus H=4q p log2(1/p)+O_q(p), while h2(pu)=u p log2(1/p)+O_q(p). Subtraction gives E=4q(1-2q)p log2(1/p)+O_q(p), exactly A046's statement. At q=1/4 the coefficient is 1/2; the identity 4q(1-2q)=1/2-8(q-1/4)^2 identifies the maximal coefficient only within this fixed-q AND family. Applying any universal tau to these actual gates and dividing by p log2(1/p) proves the claimed liminf necessity. No unreviewed uncertainty theorem is used.

Explicit contributed bound: set q=1/4, L=log2(1/p), 0<p<=1/2. The masses reduce to a=(1-p/2)^2, b=p(1-p)/4, c=3p^2/4, d=3p(1-p)/4. The following elementary bounds include their constants:

* Let s=1-a=p-p^2/4<=p. Then -a log2(a)<=s/ln2<=2p; ln2>=1/2 follows by integrating 1/t on [1,2].
* Both b/p and d/p are at least 1/8 and at most 1; therefore the b,d contributions are at most p(1-p)L+3p.
* The c contribution equals (3p^2/4)(2L+log2(4/3)) and is at most (3p^2/2)L+3p^2/4. The elementary maximum -p ln p=1/e, together with e>=2 and ln2>=1/2, gives pL<=1.

Adding these bounds gives H<=pL+(47/8)p<=pL+6p. Meanwhile h2(3p/4)>=(3p/4)log2(4/(3p))>=(3p/4)L and h2(3/4)<=1. Therefore E=2h2(3p/4)-H-p h2(3/4)>=p(L/2-7). Choosing integer k>2K+14 proves E>Kp for p=2^(-k) without computing or extrapolating an entropy sign. This is a quantitative corollary contributed separately from the unchanged accepted A046 version; A092 does not independently accept its own new lemma.

Aligned disjoint products have product spectral distributions and product physical channels, so M and J add. For m copies at the finite witness biases, the excess exceeds m*1195/10000 bits. This validates A046's tensor scope discussion but provides no shared-variable or arbitrary composition additivity.

Scope guard: under the uniform measure the same two-bit sign has four Fourier masses 1/4, H=2 and I=1. Disjoint products have H=2m, I=m and ratio exactly 2. Varying biased p changes the measure and the helper being tested. It does not produce an actual uniform-cube family with unbounded H/I.

First unsupported line in the universal route remains: pay the complete physical stopping/restriction/composition cost, including terminal entropy and each biased tax only once, by a finite dimension-independent influence budget. The endpoint obstruction and its quantitative corollary do not establish that payment. For A071, the first unverified audit obligation here is reconstruction of its entire grid coverage, sign enclosures and all equality certificates; one successful calibration does not discharge it.

# exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

Verdict: ACCEPT_AT_STATED_SCOPE for A047-SHARP-FIXED-CHILD-RARE-SELECTOR@1 from run 20261011T220210Z-fleet05-A047-boundary-search. For every fixed finite pair of Boolean children g,h in a common orthonormal product Fourier basis with fixed interior data biases, an independent physical selector chooses h with probability p and g otherwise. Writing c=E(gh), G_T=ghat(T), H_T=hhat(T), and Z=sum_{G_T=0} H_T^2,

    lim_{p->0} (J_X-M_X)/(p log2(1/p))
       =2Z+2c-2 <= 1/2-2(c-1/2)^2 <=1/2.

Here M_X=H(S_X|S_Y), while J_X=I(X;S_Y) in the actual physical-child spectral experiment. All entropy is in bits. Equality 1/2 is attained by actual constant/quarter-defect children and by balanced children after multiplication by a fresh fair character. Disjoint child Fourier supports give M_X=J_X=h2(p) exactly at every p in (0,1).

The precise refuted statement is a universal scalar tax tau(p) with endpoint coefficient below 1/2 in J_X<=M_X+tau(p). The statement is refuted even for balanced children and a typical dictator. # complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

Reconstruct the physical Boolean function directly as F(0,y)=g(y), F(1,y)=h(y). Its orthonormal selector basis consists of 1 and (X-p)/sqrt(p(1-p)). Hence each residual Fourier label T has selector-sector masses

    a_T=((1-p)G_T+pH_T)^2,
    b_T=p(1-p)(H_T-G_T)^2,
    q_T=a_T+b_T=(1-p)G_T^2+pH_T^2.

Each child law is normalized by Boolean Parseval. The physical mixture law q is exactly the residual spectral marginal, with actual branch weights 1-p,p. Therefore

    J=H(q)-(1-p)H(G^2)-pH(H^2),
    M=H((a_T,b_T)_T)-H(q).

For the finite coefficient lists, partition labels into G_T=0 and G_T!=0. On a nonzero G label, q_T and a_T converge to the positive constant G_T^2, so their entropy changes are O(p). The b mass has leading coefficient (H_T-G_T)^2 times p; if that difference is zero then b is identically zero. On a zero G label, q_T=p H_T^2, b_T=p(1-p)H_T^2, and a_T=p^2 H_T^2. Thus H(q) has p log2(1/p) coefficient Z, and H(a,b) has coefficient Z+D, where D=sum_{G_T!=0}(H_T-G_T)^2. Terms of order p^2 contribute O(p^2 log(1/p))=O(p), and the averaged fixed child entropies change only linearly. These facts prove J=Zp log2(1/p)+O_{g,h,bias}(p), M=Dp log2(1/p)+O_{g,h,bias}(p), without exchanging an infinite sum and a limit.

Parseval and correlation yield D+Z=||H-G||_2^2=2-2c, hence Z-D=2Z+2c-2. Let P_G be projection onto the nonzero labels of G. Since ||G||_2=1, c=<G,P_G H>, and Cauchy-Schwarz gives c^2<=||P_G H||_2^2=1-Z. Consequently

    2Z+2c-2 <= -2c^2+2c
       =1/2-2(c-1/2)^2 <=1/2.

Equality at the final upper bound occurs precisely when c=1/2 and P_G H=G/2. Both implications follow from equality in Cauchy-Schwarz and the completed square. The inequality itself uses only finite real unit coefficient vectors; Booleanity is needed to ensure the actual physical construction and attainability, rather than being an extra source of this upper bound.

For an explicit actual witness, take fair data bits and g=1, h=-1 only at (0,0), +1 at the other three vertices. Then c=1/2 and H=(1/2,-1/2,-1/2,-1/2) in a Walsh sign convention; Z=3/4 and D=1/4. The debt coefficient is 1/2. Multiplication of both functions by a fresh independent fair character shifts all Fourier labels bijectively, preserves the full selector ledger, and makes both children balanced. The finite balanced witness reconstructed below uses g=z and h=z except at the two vertices with y_1=y_2=0, where its sign is reversed. This gives a typical dictator and the same coefficient.

For disjoint supports, the residual label identifies which physical child generated it, giving J=h2(p). In a G-only fiber the selector spectral proportion is p; in an H-only fiber it is 1-p. Their binary entropies coincide, so M=h2(p). Equal children have M=J=0. Opposite children have J=0, M=h2(4p(1-p)); these controls check the distinction between physical and spectral selector probabilities.

The unequal-bias AND corollary is also correct at its stated scope. Starting from the four actual values (1,1,1,-1), let P(X=1)=p be fixed and P(Y=1)=q->0. The Y-present marginal mass is v=4pq(1-q); its two conditional selector masses have proportions p,1-p. The X-only, Y-absent mass is 4p(1-p)q^2=O_p(q^2). Grouping the parent law gives H(parent)=h2(v)+v h2(p)+O_p(q^2 log(1/q)). The exact child mixture identity then gives

    J-M=4p(1-p)q log2((1-p)/p)+O_p(q^2 log(1/q)).

This yields a positive leading sign for fixed p<1/2 and a negative leading sign for fixed p>1/2. It is not uniform at a simultaneous moving p,q endpoint.

No unsupported line was found in the exact A047@1 claim. The first unsupported extrapolation from this endpoint proof would be replacing its pair-dependent O(p) remainder by a dimension- and bias-independent remainder, or inferring a finite-p half-tax solely from the leading coefficient. Newer B09/B10/A096 finite-p tax sources must be reconstructed under their own versions; this endpoint review neither accepts nor rejects them. The first unpaid universal bridge remains the nonreusing payment of a complete physical tree ledger with terminal entropy.

# exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

Its half-output-conditional transcript refund is valid for every finite deterministic physical singleton stopping tree under a fixed independent interior product law, with no repeated coordinate on a path. For actual output Y=f(X), transcript Pi and node weights w_v, define c_v=H(X_i|Y,history=v). Then

    sum_v w_v[tau*(p_i)-c_v/2]_+ <= I(Pi;Y)/2 <= I_phys(f)/2,
    sum_v w_v tau*(p_i) <= I_phys(f)/2+H(Pi|Y)/2.

The sharp scalar tau* is the two-basis angular uncertainty loss defined in the source. The smallest nonnegative bank coefficient lambda in a universal bound A_T<=C I_phys+lambda H(Pi|f), with finite fixed C and essential computing trees, is 1/2. This is a scoped tax/refund theorem; compatible spectral funding and terminal Fourier payment are explicitly unpaid.

New contributed claim, pending independent review: for every such stopping tree on the uniform cube, in the coherent root-spectral/physical coupling, let S0 be the root Fourier set, R=I(f)-E I(f_leaf), and C_T=H(Pi|Y)-H(Pi|S0). Then

    C_T=sum_v w_v J_v-I(Pi;Y),
    -I(Pi;Y)<=C_T<=(2/ln2)R-I(Pi;Y).

In particular [C_T]_+/2<=R/ln2. This gives a quantitative signed conversion price, not a free conditional bank. Entropies are bits, J_v is the actual physical-child/spectral mutual information, and R is consumed uniform physical influence, counted once. The new bound does not cover arbitrary additional reference side information or resets.

# complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

First reconstruct the scalar bound independently. Put r=min(p,1-p), beta=arcsin(sqrt(r)), and tau*=h2(p)-min_t[h2(sin^2 t)+h2(sin^2(t-beta))]. For two normalized real two-vector distributions in the rotated bases, let u,w be their smaller probabilities. The maximal-probability basis vectors have overlap modulus at most sqrt(1-r). Their two rank-one projectors have summed largest eigenvalue 1+that modulus; hence u+w>=delta=1-sqrt(1-r). Since each smaller probability is in [0,1/2], entropy monotonicity and concavity give h2(u)+h2(w)>=h2(delta)>=h2(r/2)>=h2(r)/2. Evaluating at a basis vector also shows the minimum is at most h2(r). Therefore 0<=tau*<=h2(p)/2 without a finite interval minimizer or block-additivity assumption.

The exact two-basis connection to actual Boolean children is also reconstructed. If their coefficients are G_T,H_T, normalize the vector (sqrt(1-p)G_T,sqrt(p)H_T) by its norm sqrt(q_T), where q_T=(1-p)G_T^2+pH_T^2. Rotation by beta gives the parent selector-sector coefficients; the two entropies of this vector in the two bases are the physical-child posterior entropy given T and the selector spectral entropy given T. Consequently J-M=h2(p)-sum_T q_T F_p(t_T)<=tau*. For p>1/2 swap physical children first. Zero fibers are unreachable. This proves the uncertainty step used in the bundled A050 ledger without a pending computer-assisted half-Delta result.

For the physical tree define j_v=I(X_i;Y|history=v)=h2(p_i)-c_v and ell_v=h2(p_i)/2-tau*(p_i)>=0. The local net tax equals j_v/2-ell_v, so its positive part is at most j_v/2. The same-tree reserve H(Y|history)/2 has expected drop j_v/2 and leaves the terminal reserve E H(Y|Pi)/2. Its telescope pays sum_v w_v j_v/2=I(Pi;Y)/2 exactly once. Deterministic coordinate choices and stopping create no extra unrecorded entropy. Thus sum_v w_v c_v=H(Pi|Y), proving both claimed refund inequalities.

Root output entropy is bounded by actual physical influence: the product-input chain rule and adding later coordinates give H(Y)<=sum_i I(Y;X_i|X_-i)=sum_i h2(p_i)Inf_i^phys(f)<=I_phys(f). On each other-coordinate fiber the Boolean function is constant or a literal, which proves the equality with weighted pivotal probabilities. This argument pays output information only; it does not replace spectral energy by physical influence in a biased FEI claim.

The bank coefficient is sharp. At the midpoint angle, s=(1-sqrt(1-p))/2=p/4+O(p^2), so tau*(p)>=h2(p)-2h2(s). Combined with the half-entropy upper bound this gives tau*/h2(p)->1/2. If lambda<1/2, fix q with tau*(q)>lambda h2(q). For the actual Boolean point defect f_r=1-2 product_{i=1}^r X_i under bias q, every essential computing tree follows the all-one chain until a zero is seen. Its expected queries equal (1-q^r)/(1-q), its physical influence is r q^(r-1), and H(Pi|f_r)=h2(q)(1-q^r)/(1-q)-h2(q^r). Therefore A_T-lambda H(Pi|f_r) tends to the positive constant [tau*(q)-lambda h2(q)]/(1-q), while physical influence tends to zero. This refutes precisely every fixed-C inequality with lambda<1/2; it is not a uniform-cube FEI counterfamily.

Independent finite sign: use A050's exact original q=1-((2^128-1)/2^128)^2, lambda=49/100 and r=4. Then the midpoint smaller mass is exactly s=2^-129. From the original sixteen-entry table (+1 except at 1111), independently compute all Fourier squares, output probability q^4 and influence 4q^3. A rational log enclosure certifies

    [h2(q)-2h2(s)](1+q+q^2+q^3)
       -(49/100)[h2(q)(1+q+q^2+q^3)-h2(q^4)] >4q^3.

Because the displayed midpoint loss is a lower bound on tau*, this is a rigorous actual tax violation with C=1. The all-C/all-lambda necessity is supplied by the analytic argument, not by extrapolating this one case.

The source's terminal-inclusive spectral identity is correct. Set M_T=sum_v w_v M_v and S_T=sum_v w_v[h2(p_i)/2-(J_v-M_v)]. Chain rules give

    Hhat(f)=E Hhat(f_leaf)+2M_T+H(Pi|Y)/2+I(Pi;Y)/2-S_T.

No optimization of tau* is needed: ell_v+D_v equals the bracket defining S_T. S_T is nonnegative by the angular proof. Its terminal Fourier term is separate from the output reserve. At fair physical bias tau*=0, so the transcript entropy cancels the matching ell term; there is no positive fair-coordinate scalar tax.

New quantitative conversion proof: reconstruct the function-specific coherent coupling. At a uniform node with residual label T, parent fiber mass q_T and physical child coefficient C_b(T), the branch kernel is C_b(T)^2/(2q_T), discarding the queried spectral label. Boolean Parseval gives the actual fair physical branches and actual child squared spectra. The kernel depends only on the retained T; removed root labels have no extra information about that branch conditional on T and history. Thus the root-spectral information release at this node is exactly J_v, and I(S0;Pi)=sum_v w_v J_v. For a partial stopping tree, extend the coupling to a finite computing tree solely to attach the actual Y; its stopped prefix retains the same marginal law and the same information identity.

Subtract the two conditional entropy chain rules:

    H(Pi|Y)-H(Pi|S0)=I(S0;Pi)-I(Y;Pi).

Nonnegativity of J gives the lower bound. At each uniform node the elementary scalar JS inequality d(z)<=1-sqrt(1-z^2), with d'(z)=atanh(z), yields J_v<=(2/ln2)Inf_i(f_v). To verify the scalar inequality, compare derivatives: the derivative of z/sqrt(1-z^2)-atanh(z) is (1-z^2)^(-3/2)-(1-z^2)^(-1)>=0. Integrating twice and using continuity at the endpoints proves it. The physical influence removal identity gives sum_v w_v Inf_i(f_v)=R=I(f)-E I(f_leaf). Summing proves the claimed conversion bound and its positive-half-bank price R/ln2. This is a new quantitative corollary of the reconstructed common lift and the established scalar JS mechanism; independent acceptance of this contributed version is pending.

Two actual fair controls show the signed price cannot be silently dropped: the one-bit character gives conversion -1; the forward essential two-bit AND gives conversion -1+(3/4)log2(3)=1-h2(1/4)>0. A common-space tree can therefore use a bound with its explicit cost, but cannot assume either conditional bank dominates the other.

First unsupported line remains: an actual normalized compatible spectral reference supplies H(Pi|S0) or the needed witness-conditioned bank once, including any reference-reset or additional side-information corrections, and pays the signed M_T/S_T remainder and terminal Fourier entropy from the remaining exclusive influence budget. Our conversion bound does not supply that bank. Substituting its exact signed identity into A050's residual simply recovers Hhat(f)=E Hhat(f_leaf)+M_T+J_T. It cannot be counted as a second independent influence refund. No arbitrary witness label is identified with S0 without a separate compatibility proof.

## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

Independent reconstruction of the B09 specialization follows. Set B=A^c, c_{R,T}=fhat_p(R union T), q_T=sum_R c_{R,T}². Physical Parseval gives q_T=sum_z P_A(z) fhat_z(T)². Booleanity gives each child total squared mass one, so the physical marginal is exactly P_A and J_A=I(Z;T)>=0. The spectral chain rule gives L_A=M_A+J_A.

For q_T>0, let a_R=c_{R,T}/sqrt(q_T) and b_z=sqrt(P_A(z)) fhat_z(T)/sqrt(q_T). Both vectors have squared norm one. Product-basis evaluation gives b=Ua where each one-bit factor is [[sqrt(1-p),-sqrt(p)],[sqrt(p),sqrt(1-p)]]. Its columns are orthonormal, and c=max|U_zR|=product_i sqrt(max(p_i,1-p_i)). The primary uncertainty theorem applies to these two finite orthonormal bases. Averaging its entropy lower bound gives M_A+H(Z|T)>=-sum_i log2 max(p_i,1-p_i). Since H(Z)=sum_i h2(p_i), subtraction yields exactly the claimed bound. A empty is the identity experiment, with all terms zero. No dimension, symmetry, gate-primality or weight regularity assumption was used. This accepts the core v1 at its stated scope without a substantive repair to its proof.

The first unpaid mathematical implication is global, nonreusing influence payment of 2M_A+Delta_A and terminal entropy. Neither uncertainty nor marginal entropy subadditivity supplies that payment. # A093 B19: restriction priority and a GMP constant correction

## exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

For every Boolean f:{-1,1}^n->{-1,1} under uniform measure, H is Shannon entropy of squared Walsh coefficients in bits, and I_i=sum_{S containing i} fhat(S)^2. Audit the prior occurrence of H(f)<=2|A|+E H(f restricted on A) and the arbitrary-child one-coordinate decrement identity; compare the local all-LTF Theorem A with corrected primary literature. A093 follows the B19 lead's named target. Full FEI remains OPEN; this stage establishes no priority superlative.

## sources actually read, revisions and theorem locators

See PRIMARY_SOURCE_OVERLAP.md for the nine named primary papers, URLs, exact statements, and locators. The core positive overlap is CKLS ECCC revision 2, Lemma 3.2 (PDF p. 9), and GMP26 v2, Appendix (32) (PDF p. 28). GMP stopping-tree Ansatz 1.2 is on pp. 16–17. The v1 appendix was also read to check whether v2 repaired the constant issue; it did not. PDF page 16 was rendered and visually inspected, confirming that the false binary-entropy inequality is printed, rather than a text-extraction error. Primary ECCC correction and Han/GMP arXiv version pages were checked. BOARD.md (supervisor stamp 2026-10-13 23:49 UTC), B19 STREAM_NOTES.md v2, own STATUS.md, and execution protocol revision 2026-10-11-execution-v2 were consumed. No external trees were modified. No artifacts survived from the interrupted 20261012 stage; earlier completed B09 work was not repeated.

## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

Write a_T=fhat_plus(T), b_T=fhat_minus(T), q_T=(a_T^2+b_T^2)/2, and h=h2. The parent coefficients are (a_T+b_T)/2 and (a_T-b_T)/2. For q_T>0 set u_T=(a_T-b_T)^2/[2(a_T^2+b_T^2)] and t_T=a_T^2/(a_T^2+b_T^2). Direct entropy grouping gives

Delta_i H = sum_T q_T [h(u_T)+1-h(t_T)] = E_i+R_i,

where E_i=sum q_T h(u_T) is GMP's (22) quantity, and R_i=sum q_T[1-h(t_T)] >=0. Zero fibers contribute zero. This is its Appendix (32); it translates to C005/C006. Since sum q_T=1, both E_i and R_i are <=1, proving 0<=Delta_i H<=2. Applying this at each of |A| coordinates and averaging over earlier uniform restrictions yields the block +2|A| inequality. CKLS already states the singleton bound explicitly; the block extension is its elementary iteration.

The exact witness for the same-K replacement in GMP Corollary 4.2 is f(x,y)=-1 except f(1,1)=1. Its four coefficients are (-1/2,1/2,1/2,1/2), so H(f)=2. Split x: f_plus=y and f_minus=-1 both have entropy 0, and I_x=1/2. The two occupied child fibers have coefficient pairs (0,-1) and (1,0), hence E_x=1, R_x=1 and Delta_x=2. At K=2, (22) holds, while (21) fails. This is an actual Boolean witness to a source's same-constant substitution, not a counterexample to FEI: total influence is 1 and H/I=2.

A safe general repair is E_i<=K I_i implies Delta_i H<=(K+4)I_i. Here is a self-contained bound on the refund. For t=(1+e)/2, |e|<=1, the entropy series is

1-h(t) = (1/ln 2) sum_{k>=1} e^{2k}/[2k(2k-1)] <= e^2,

because all terms are nonnegative and their coefficients sum to ln 2, as follows at e=1 by continuity/monotone convergence. Therefore q(1-h(a^2/(a^2+b^2))) <= (a^2-b^2)^2/[2(a^2+b^2)] <= (a-b)^2. Summing gives R_i<=sum(a-b)^2=4I_i. Conversely Delta_i<=K I_i implies E_i<=K I_i. Existence of a dimension-independent bound is therefore equivalent after allowing a constant loss; exact same-K substitution is not.

The appendix's first unsupported implication is its assertion after (32) that a bounded discarded term gives an if-and-only-if with the asserted constants. It changes from individual influence to sum(a-b)^2 without preserving the factor 1/4. If E_i<=K' sum(a-b)^2 then this derivation gives Delta_i<=4(K'+1)I_i, not the printed K=K'+1 in the influence normalization. A separate printed error is equation (20): h(t)<=sqrt(t(1-t)) fails at t=1/2, where the sides are exactly 1 and 1/2. Consequently arguments using that displayed bound or Corollary 4.3 require their own constant reconstruction. This stage does not refute all claimed restricted-class theorems. The telescope using the true decrement in Ansatz 1.2 remains valid.

The all-LTF comparison is limited to read statements: CKLS corrected H=O(sqrt n), CKKLS random-weight and small-weight regular subclasses, Han's influence-entropy sum bound, Potapov's flat-spectrum classes, Chang's biased lower bound, and Fang–Wang's low-degree Fourier weight. None of these read statements asserts the local Theorem A. Their absence from this table does not certify global priority or best constants. Historical Han overlap acknowledgment and CKLS correction matter for citations; the old uncorrected all-LTF statement is not a valid established comparator.

The concrete substitution uses A008's allowed normalized single-gate uniform nonempty dictionary on N data coordinates. For balanced g, apply the A012 compatible reference transfer. It has full-output masses (1-p)^2 at vacuum, p^2 at every nonempty control-only set, and p^2 R_g(T) for nonempty data. Its exact cost is p c_g+A_r. For a half-mass core trial, normalization plus sector KL equals exactly -p log2 z; for a normalized core dictionary, both vanish, as do both conditional control KL terms. The remaining whole debt is exactly p[log2(2^N-1)-H(g)]. The direct uniform dictionary on N+r coordinates also has an additional explicitly calculated binary-sector KL; compatible transfer removes that term, not the data debt.

Let g=z0 times t disjoint width-L point-defect signs, with t=4^L, N=tL+1 and r=4L+1. Then I(F)=2^(-4L-1)[2L2^L+4L+2]->0, minority probability tends to zero, and the transferred uniform-dictionary cost C satisfies C/I>=2^(L-2). The actual Boolean entropy obeys H(F)<4I(F), so (C-H)/I>2^(L-2)-4 tends to infinity. A fixed explicit occupied-block/local-subset reference equals the same family's product spectrum and has zero debt. This comparison is not a new FEI class theorem; it isolates which structural reference choice succeeds and which payment proposal fails.

The difficult additional regime is a flip that destroys original edges. For an arbitrary Boolean G and vertex a with j(a) originally pivotal incident edges, exact physical surgery gives I(H_a)=I(G)+2*2^-n[n-2j(a)] and I(M_a)=I(G)+2^-n[n+1-2j(a)].     C_pt=(16/29)*[8-(180log2(3)+300log2(5))/256],
    2.297<C_pt<2.299.

# A095: ordered pivotal-height criterion and the rank/KL frontier

## exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

Full FEI remains OPEN. This stage extracts two complete statements, with independent acceptance pending for the new statements. It also independently reconstructs B19-RANKCODE-REDUCTION v1 and B19-RANKCODE-GLOBAL-ORDER-NOGO v1. No novelty or priority claim is made.

Let f:{-1,1}^n -> {-1,1} have the uniform Fourier law P(S)=fhat(S)^2. H is in bits, I=sum_i Inf_i=E|S|, and ln and all KL quantities explicitly marked D_nat use natural logarithms. A coordinate order sigma has positions 1,...,n; pi below denotes the numerical circle constant, never a coordinate order. Put

    R_sigma(f)=E_P sum_{j=1}^{|S|} ln(i_j/j),

where i_1<...<i_|S| are the positions in sigma of S. Empty sets contribute zero. Write A=log2(4+e)+log2(pi^2/4).

Statement 1 (physical criterion). For each coordinate i at position p, let U_i be its p-1 predecessors. Fixing the remaining coordinates of the derivative cube gives a fiber of supp(d_i f) on U_i. Suppose EACH such fiber admits an orientation of U_i in which its longest strictly increasing chain has at most r_i points, for specified integers r_i>=1. Orientations may vary between coordinates AND between fixed-future fibers. Then

    H(f) <= A I(f) + (2/ln2) sum_i Inf_i(f) L(r_i),
    L(r)=min{r(1+ln2), 2+gamma+2 ln r}.

In particular r_i<=r gives a dimension-independent FEI constant A+(2/ln2)L(r); r=1 recovers C_reg exactly. More generally sum_i Inf_i ln r_i <= K I gives

    H(f) <= [A+(2/ln2)(2+gamma+2K)] I(f).

For fixed uniform r the class is closed under arbitrary physical restrictions, using the induced coordinate order. This is an intrinsic pivotal-support condition; it assumes neither unateness nor a representation as a Boolean combination of regular gates. It is a sufficient class criterion, not a characterization of all FEI functions or a claim to have found a maximal natural class. Its overlap with existing representation theorems has not been classified.

Statement 2 (exact equivalence with reference credit). For a fixed sigma, let

    Z_(n,k)=sum_{|S|=k} prod_{i in positions(S)} i^(-2),
    Q_(sigma,k)(S)=Z_(n,k)^(-1) prod_{i in positions(S)} i^(-2),
    beta_(n,k)=ln(Z_(n,k)(k!)^2),
    D_sigma(f)=sum_k P(|S|=k) D_nat(P(S | |S|=k) || Q_(sigma,k)),
    T_sigma(f)=2 R_sigma(f)-D_sigma(f).

At k=0, Q assigns mass one to the empty set, Z=1 and beta=0; zero-mass levels are omitted. For every Boolean f and EVERY order sigma,

    (ln2) H(f) = H_nat(|S|)+T_sigma(f)+E beta_(n,|S|),
    0 <= beta_(n,k) <= k ln(pi^2/4).

Let C_FEI=sup_f H(f)/I(f), over nonconstant Boolean functions in all dimensions, and let K_T=sup_f inf_sigma T_sigma(f)/I(f). In the extended real sense,

    K_T/ln2 <= C_FEI <= A+K_T/ln2.

Thus finiteness of K_T is equivalent to FEI. The exact missing universal statement is T_sigma<=K I for some order at each f, with the complete normalized KL credit retained. This equivalence does not bound K_T.

## sources actually read, revisions and theorem locators

Execution protocol fei-execution-protocol/2, revision 2026-10-11-execution-v2, was read in full. Campaign README, CURRENT_STATUS, claim/obstruction/packet registries, stream next actions, plan .tex, CAMPAIGN, CLAIMS, COUNTEREXAMPLES, SEED_LEMMAS and NATIVE_MAPPING were consulted. BOARD snapshot 2026-10-11 23:09 UTC and B19 STREAM_NOTES v1 (22:41 UTC) supplied the target and duplication guard. Full large plan/index reads exceeded terminal output limits; this report does not pretend that every unrelated packet was substantively audited.

Earlier 29-page version was checked for its analogous locators and different one-order reference; the exact reduction uses the 38-page revision. The supplied percolation 30-page text, Theorems 1.1-1.2 and introduction, and theorem locators in its 31-page version, were inspected for scope only; no percolation theorem is a premise. ## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

### 1. Universal rank reduction, independently reconstructed

For each k, Euler's product gives

    sum_{|S|=k} prod_{i in positions(S)} i^(-2)
       <= pi^(2k)/(2k+1)!.

This follows by comparing the coefficient of z^k in prod_{i>=1}(1+z/i^2)=sinh(pi sqrt(z))/(pi sqrt(z)); finite n only decreases the coefficient. Therefore the reference

    Q'_k(S)=((2k+1)!/pi^(2k)) prod_{i in positions(S)} i^(-2)

is a subprobability on k-sets. For S with ordered positions i_j,

    -ln Q'_k(S)=2 sum_j ln(i_j/j)
                   + ln(pi^(2k)(k!)^2/(2k+1)!).

The final logarithm is at most k ln(pi^2/4): (2k+1)!>=4^k(k!)^2, since its ratio to the right side starts at one and successive ratios are (2k+3)/(2k+2)>=1. Gibbs on each level gives

    H(S | |S|) <= (2/ln2) R_sigma + log2(pi^2/4) I.

For completeness, the Boolean spectral-level estimate is H(|S|)<=log2(4+e) I. Here is its full scalar reduction from cube edge-isoperimetry. Let p=min Pr(f=1),Pr(f=-1), v=4p(1-p)=Pr(|S|>0). Edge-isoperimetry gives I>=2p log2(1/p). This implies

    I >= v+(v/2) log2(1/v).

To verify the comparison put q=1-2p. The difference between its two sides is (1-q)/2 times

    phi(q)=(q-1)log2(1-q)+(1+q)log2(1+q)-2q.

phi(0)=phi(1)=0 and phi''(q)=-2q/((1-q^2)ln2)<=0, so phi>=0. Consequently

    h2(v)<=2I+(log2(e)-2)v.

The geometric reference (1-rho)rho^(k-1) on positive k, with rho=4/(4+e), gives

    v H(|S| | |S|>0) <= (I-v)log2(1/rho)+v log2(1/(1-rho)).

Its v coefficient cancels the one in the binary-entropy bound. The chain rule proves the asserted level estimate. v=0 is immediate and endpoints follow by continuity. Combining both bounds proves B19-RANKCODE-REDUCTION v1 with no regularity hypothesis. Cube edge-isoperimetry, Gibbs, and Euler's classical product are explicit standard premises; no unreviewed campaign claim is needed.

### 2. Bounded chain height pays the rank cost

We first prove a signed derivative lemma. Let d:{-1,1}^N->{0,+1,-1}, alpha=E d^2>0, and U have M coordinates. Suppose each U-fiber of supp(d) has increasing-chain height at most r in some orientation of that fiber. A finite poset of height at most r partitions into r antichains: assign each point the length of the longest chain ending there; comparable distinct points have strictly different labels. Every restriction to a subcube retains this property. Sperner therefore bounds support density in every q-dimensional subcube by

    min{1,r c_q}, c_q=2^(-q) binom(q,floor(q/2)) <= (q+1)^(-1/2).

Resample each U-coordinate at rate t. Given X and the mask, Y is uniform in a subcube of dimension B~Bin(M,t). Its orientation is inherited from the fixed outside-U fiber; it may depend on that fiber. Since |d|=d^2 and the mask is independent of X,

    E[d(X)d(Y)] <= E[|d(X)||d(Y)|]
                    <= alpha E min{1,r c_B}.

Parseval and the resampling identity give, for nu(T)=dhat(T)^2/alpha and kappa=|T intersect U|,

    E_nu (1-t)^kappa <= E min{1,r c_B}
                      <= r/sqrt((M+1)t).

The final step uses Cauchy-Schwarz and

    E[1/(B+1)]=(1-(1-t)^(M+1))/((M+1)t).

This argument allows arbitrary signs of d and independent fiber orientations. It never replaces a coherent signed spectrum by an incoherent mixture.

Set p=M+1, Z=(kappa+1)/p and G=-ln Z. Substituting t=1-exp(-a/p) gives

    E_nu exp(-a Z) <= min{1,r a^(-1/2)}, a>0,

because 1-exp(-s)>=s exp(-s). Frullani and 0<Z<=1 then imply

    E G = integral_0^infinity [E exp(-aZ)-exp(-a)] da/a
        <= integral_0^(r^2) (1-exp(-a)) da/a
           + integral_(r^2)^infinity (r a^(-1/2)-exp(-a)) da/a
         = 2 ln r+gamma+2.

The integral identity follows from integral_0^A(1-exp(-a))da/a - integral_A^infinity exp(-a)da/a = ln A+gamma. Nonnegative integration justifies averaging Frullani; all variables are finite here.

An alternative exact-density argument improves small r. Let h_k=sum_{j=1}^k 1/j. The harmonic integral yields

    E h_kappa >= sum_{q=1}^M (1-r c_q)/q,
    E G <= h_M-E h_kappa <= r sum_{q=1}^M c_q/q <= r(1+ln2).

The first inequality may have negative summands, which is harmless: it comes from the valid upper bound E(1-t)^kappa<=r E c_B. The beta integrals are binom(M,q) integral_0^1 t^(q-1)(1-t)^(M-q)dt=1/q. Pointwise ln((M+1)/(k+1))<=h_M-h_k proves the middle step. Finally c_(2j-1)=c_(2j)=b_j=4^(-j)binom(2j,j), and sum_j b_j/(2j-1)=1, sum_j b_j/(2j)=ln2. These follow by integrating the binomial generating functions: sum b_j z^j/(2j-1)=1-sqrt(1-z), and integral_0^1((1-z)^(-1/2)-1)dz/(2z)=ln2. M=0 gives G=0 directly. This proves E G<=L(r).

For d=d_i f and U=U_i, its normalized squared spectrum is the law of S minus i conditioned on i in S. Hence

    R_sigma(f)=sum_i Inf_i(f) E_(nu_i) ln(position(i)/(1+|T intersect U_i|))
                <=sum_i Inf_i(f) L(r_i).

Combining with Section 1 proves Statement 1. For fixed r, physical restrictions only intersect original pivotal supports with faces and remove fixed coordinates from the order; chain height and the allowed fiber-dependent orientations survive. Thus the restricted class is hereditary.

An explicit unbounded dimension family satisfying r<=2 is

    F(z,y)=g_a(y) when z=+1, and -g_b(y) when z=-1,
    g_t(y)=+1 iff the number of positive y_j is at least t,

with z first, then any data order, and 1<=a,b<=d. For a data derivative its support at each selector value is a Hamming level on the remaining prefix data. Each of the two selector layers is an antichain, so a chain has at most two pivotal assignments. For the first selector derivative the predecessor cube is empty. This covers growing, biased, signed examples, although it also overlaps existing regular-gate composition results. The 54 saved exact examples verify this physical hypothesis directly. No novelty is inferred from those examples.

### 3. Exact boundary: XOR of AND blocks

Let g_L=1-2*1_{x=x0} on L bits (a sign convention of AND or its negation) and f_(m,L)=prod_{b=1}^m g_L on disjoint blocks. Put p=2^(-L), q=2p. Its one-block squared spectrum is

    P(empty)=(1-2p)^2, P(S)=4p^2 for nonempty S,
    Inf_i=q, I(g_L)=Lq.

Tensor spectra imply H(f)=m H(g_L), I(f)=m Lq. For L>=2,

    H(g_L)=-(1-2p)^2 log2((1-2p)^2)
             +8p(1-p)(L-1).

Using -a ln a<=1-a=4p(1-p) gives

    H(g_L)/I(g_L)
       <= (1-p)[4(L-1)/L+2/(L ln2)] < 4.

The strict final comparison follows from 2/ln2<4. Thus this entire obstruction family itself satisfies FEI with constant 4.

Fix ANY order. Given that coordinate c at position u is in S, other coordinates in its own block are independently present with probability 1/2, and other blocks retain their unconditional law. If b preceding coordinates share its block, and N_u is the number of occupied positions at most u, then

    E[N_u | c in S]=1+b/2+q(u-1-b)
       <= (L+1)/2+q(u-1), for L>=2.

Convexity of -ln and N_u<=u yield

    R_sigma(f) >= q sum_(u=1)^(mL)
        max{0,ln u-ln((L+1)/2+q(u-1))}.

This is exactly the quantitative bound in B19-RANKCODE-GLOBAL-ORDER-NOGO v1. For fixed L, the summand tends to ln(1/q), so its Cesaro average gives

    liminf_(m->infinity) inf_sigma R_sigma(f)/(mLq)
         >= ln(1/q)=(L-1)ln2.

As a direct diagonal bound, E N_u<=L+q u. For u>=2^L L, this is at most 3*2^(-L)u. For m>2^L and L>=2,

    R_sigma(f)/I(f) >= (1-2^L/m)(L ln2-ln3).

Taking m=2^(2L) proves divergence uniformly over all orders, while H/I<4. This refutes only a universal estimate R_sigma<=K I with one global order. It does not refute FEI, adaptive references, normalized mixtures of orders with paid selection, or reference-KL credit. Sections 1 and 3 independently accept both lead v1 claims at their stated scope; the report supplies its own all-parameter reconstruction rather than relying on the lead's absent REPORT.md.

### 4. The exact KL credit and equivalence

Normalize the finite Euler weight on each level as in Statement 2. It is strictly positive on every k-set. Its surprisal is exactly

    -ln Q_(sigma,k)(S)=2 sum_j ln(i_j/j)+beta_(n,k).

The k-set consisting of the first k positions contributes 1/(k!)^2 to Z_(n,k), giving beta>=0. The Euler coefficient bound and factorial comparison from Section 1 give beta<=k ln(pi^2/4). Since cross-entropy equals entropy plus KL,

    H_nat(S | |S|)=2R_sigma+E beta-D_sigma.

The chain rule proves Statement 2's exact identity. In particular T_sigma<=H_nat(S) for every order. FEI(C) implies T_sigma<=C ln2 I. Conversely, if each f has an order with T_sigma<=K I, the level-entropy estimate and beta upper bound give H<= [A+K/ln2] I. There are finitely many orders at every n, so the infimum is a minimum. Taking suprema proves the stated two-sided comparison. K_T>=0 since every nonconstant character has H=T=0; no issue arises from negative T at some functions.

On the obstruction family, 2R_sigma/I diverges but the identity and H/I<4 force

    D_sigma >= 2R_sigma-4 ln2 I,
    T_sigma <= 4 ln2 I.

Thus discarding KL removes exactly the compensation needed on the cleanest product obstruction. Bounding T for arbitrary Boolean functions is still FEI-equivalent; using this identity alone is not progress on its universal value. What is new in this stage's extraction is the source-complete physical class criterion and the explicit retained-credit target, not a universal estimate.

First unsupported line for a universal extension: there exists an absolute K such that every Boolean f admits sigma with 2R_sigma(f)-D_sigma(f)<=K I(f). Neither the physical-height theorem nor tensor additivity proves this outside their stated hypotheses.

# A095 stage 2: a paid block reference through a common control spine

## exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

Full FEI remains OPEN. Following B19 STREAM_NOTES v2, this stage realizes a fixed normalized reference for XORs of disjoint AND/point-defect blocks, then consumes A094's requested one-coordinate-overlap objection. The extension allows an arbitrary number of unequal private blocks sharing the same conjunction of r>=1 controls. It preserves a supplied arbitrary terminal core reference explicitly.

Entropy, cross-entropy CE(P,R)=sum P log2(1/R), and KL debt are bits; scalar analytic ln uses nats. References are probability laws, fixed across spectral outputs, positive on the actual target support. Costs are ideal negative-log costs with a terminating finite reference decoder. No rounding bit is claimed paid for an optional one-symbol prefix code.

Let q be any Boolean terminal on fresh variables with normalized reference R_q and let h_j=1-2 A_(L_j) on disjoint private blocks, L_j>=1. Define g=q product_j h_j, mu=E g, p=2^-r, F=1+A_r(g-1). Empty-calibrate R_q to match (E q)^2 at empty; its CE, denoted C_q_cal, does not increase. The fixed block reference and its biased activation transfer satisfy

    CE_F <= p C_q_cal +4p sum_j I(h_j)+6rp(1-mu),
    I(F)=p[I(q)+sum_j I(h_j)+r(1-mu)].

If the supplied terminal cost obeys CE_q<=C I(q), then CE_F<=max(C,6)I(F). This conditional statement leaves arbitrary terminal entropy and debt intact. With q=1, the entire explicit shared-spine class has CE_F<=6I(F), uniformly in number of blocks, unequal widths, r and common read. With r=0 and q=1, the disjoint product reference has exact cost H(g)<4I(g). These are complete reference-cost results on specified classes; their underlying FEI conclusions overlap existing composition results, and no novelty or priority claim is made.

The full transfer is stronger than an entropy bound: for any biased Boolean g and normalized reference R, calibrate it to R0(empty)=mu^2. Then the output reference Q is normalized, its entire data debt is retained, and

    CE(P_F,Q)-H(F)=p[CE(P_g,R0)-H(g)].

No forest generator identity is asserted: this is a new explicit output reference, not an uncharged substitution into PI-J.

## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

DERIVATION.md supplies the complete reference construction, normalization, decoder, debt identity, six-influence scalar bound, iteration over r controls, local occupied-block cost, and conditional arbitrary-terminal application. Key steps are summarized here to make the mathematical delta explicit.

First calibrate the empty atom of a normalized core reference. By the KL chain rule, the entire decrease in CE is the Bernoulli divergence between the true and reference empty/nonempty sectors; conditional data debt is unchanged. Set R0(empty)=mu^2 and scale the remaining reference to total mass 1-mu^2. If that mass is zero, use the point mass. Calibration uses the instance's known scalar bias and fixed reference parameters, not a spectrum-dependent choice for each output.

The activation reference has vacuum mass [1-p(1-mu)]^2, each nonempty control-only subset mass p^2(1-mu)^2, and each nonempty data T with each control subset U mass p^2 R0(T). It sums to one. In the data sector a finite generator draws nonempty data from R0 conditioned on that sector and a uniform control subset; control-only and vacuum sectors are explicit. Every label, absent block, normalization and local subset is included. The actual spectrum has the identical formula with P_g(T) in place of R0(T). Expanding CE-H gives exactly p D(P_g||R0). Thus both the bias sectors and all conditional control debts are fixed, and no arbitrary terminal bill has disappeared.

For one control, put t=(1-|mu|)/2. Exact conditional erasure contributes 2t(1-t) from data plus the entropy packet on masses (1-t)^2,t^2. ln(1+x)<=x bounds this packet, and the scalar JS inequality gives JS<=2t/ln2. Optimizing the resulting one-variable upper bound proves

    H(F)-H(g)/2 <= [2+(2+exp(-1/2))/ln2]t <6t<=6 Inf_control.

The strict constant uses only ln2>2/3 and e>9/4, both proved by positive elementary series. DERIVATION reconstructs the scalar JS inequality directly; no unreviewed campaign claim is needed. Inserting the exact retained debt yields CE_F<=CE_g_cal/2+6 Inf_control. Iteration over fresh controls gives p CE_g_cal+6rp(1-mu), with precisely the same direct reference formula.

The private block reference chooses occupied blocks independently with probabilities 4*2^-L_j(1-2^-L_j), then draws a uniform nonempty local subset. Its probabilities are explicit rational functions of widths and equal the squared spectrum of the specified defect blocks by direct Fourier expansion. Occupancy labels cost sum h2(delta_j)<=2 sum I(h_j); local subset cost is <2 sum I(h_j). This proves the requested O(I) reference on the global-order obstruction with all labels retained. Uniform local subsets replace an unnecessary heavier rank law here; the conditional binomial predecessor law would also pay within-block ranks, as A008 already shows.

For q=1, sharing common controls gives

    product_j(1-2 A_r A_(L_j))=1+A_r(product_j h_j-1).

It therefore realizes a family of arbitrarily many gates sharing r physical coordinates, and CE<=6I, independently of read. For a general terminal q, product factorization before activation adds C_q_cal and the local block costs; its terminal component appears multiplied by p in the final bound. A bounded terminal reference is a hypothesis, not a consequence of this construction.

REVIEW_A094.md independently reconstructs A094-CONCRETE-ACTIVATED-DICTIONARY-DEBT-AND-RANK-BOUNDARY v1 at its full stated scope. The diagonal t=4^L,r=4L+1 retains C/I>=2^(L-2) for the uniform dictionary while H/I<4 and I tends to zero. The exact all-order rank limit follows from the equal-inclusion factorial identity, the block second moment, and size-biased Jensen with integral factorial error bounds. No substantive source repair was supplied, so this is an independent acceptance.

# A095 restricted-result extraction, stage 3

# A096: source-bound gap ledger and sharp fixed-pair rare-bias coefficient

## exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

Packet A096, B20. Full uniform-cube FEI remains OPEN. The nearest unpaid lemma selected is the sharp one-bit rare-bias tax in B09's section 8, with A046's AND lower-bound work treated as existing work rather than repeated. The final result is the dimension-uniform upper tax J_X-M_X<=h2(r)-h2(1-sqrt(1-r)), r=min(p,1-p), and the sharp universal rare-endpoint coefficient 1/2. Two complete local claims await independent review; no third candidate is opened.

Fix a finite residual cube with any fixed interior product measure, and two actual Boolean sign children G,H. A new independent selector X has Pr(X=1)=p, and F_p equals G at X=1 and H at X=0. All entropies are in bits and residual parameters and children are fixed as p tends to zero. Write a_T=hat G(T), b_T=hat H(T), c=E[GH], D={T:b_T !=0}, A=sum_{T in D}a_T^2. Let M_p=H(S_X|S_residual), and J_p=I(X;T) in the actual physical-child/spectral experiment. Then

    J_p-M_p = kappa(G,H) p log2(1/p) + O_{G,H,measure}(p),
    kappa = 2(c-A),
    -4 <= kappa <= 1/2,
    1/2-kappa = 2 ||P_D a-c b||_2^2 + 2(c-1/2)^2.

Consequently the supremum, over fixed actual finite Boolean child pairs, of the limiting normalized tax is exactly 1/2. Equality holds exactly when c=1/2 and P_D a=b/2. The example H=1 and G=(-1)^{y1 y2} on two uniform residual bits has c=a_empty=1/2 and realizes equality. Multiplying both children by any residual character gives nonconstant common-child examples as well. The claim is a fixed-pair endpoint theorem, not a dimension-uniform remainder bound or a full FEI theorem.

The B09 source pin is adb671ffcda143fa68347806bb96e8c305621a7d, B05 80cc2e5027efa56d79c4ecdf7d4949759ef2068b, B19 7955d427222392911dc6af2e4ee3aa8aab67ac8c. ## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

Let r_{0,T}=((1-p)b_T+p a_T)^2 and r_{1,T}=p(1-p)(a_T-b_T)^2. These are the parent squared Fourier masses without and with the selector coordinate. Their sum is

    q_T=(1-p)b_T^2+p a_T^2.

The child's spectral channel has joint probabilities (1-p)b_T^2 and p a_T^2, so its output marginal is the same q_T. Parseval gives sum a_T^2=sum b_T^2=1. Therefore

    J_p=H(q)-(1-p)H(b^2)-p H(a^2),
    M_p=sum_T q_T h2(r_{1,T}/q_T).

The entropy summand e(t)=-t log2 t, extended by e(0)=0, is differentiable at each positive t. On D, q_T=b_T^2+O(p), so these terms of H(q) change by O(p). Outside D, q_T=p a_T^2 and exactly

    e(p a_T^2)=p a_T^2 log2(1/p)+p e(a_T^2).

Because the finite pair is fixed, summing yields

    J_p = (1-A) p log2(1/p) + O(p).                 (1)

For T in D with a_T != b_T, write t_T=(a_T-b_T)^2>0. Then r_{1,T}=p t_T+O(p^2), q_T=b_T^2+O(p), and

    q_T h2(r_{1,T}/q_T)
      = r_{1,T} log2(q_T/r_{1,T}) + O(r_{1,T})
      = p t_T log2(1/p)+O(p).

Here h2(u)=u log2(1/u)+O(u) as u decreases to zero. The O(p^2 log(1/p)) error is O(p). If a_T=b_T the conditional summand is identically zero. For T outside D with a_T !=0, the two parent masses are p^2 a_T^2 and p(1-p)a_T^2, so the conditional summand is p a_T^2 h2(p)=O(p^2 log(1/p))=O(p). Zero/zero fibers contribute zero. Thus

    M_p = sum_{T in D}(a_T-b_T)^2 p log2(1/p)+O(p). (2)

Subtract (2) from (1), and use sum_D b_T^2=1 and sum_D a_T b_T=c:

    kappa=1-A-(A+1-2c)=2(c-A).                    (3)

The vector b is unit and supported on D. Orthogonal projection yields

    ||P_D a-c b||^2=A-c^2 >=0.

Hence the exact identity

    1/2-kappa=2(A-c^2)+2(c-1/2)^2                 (4)

proves kappa<=1/2, with the claimed equality conditions. Since A<=1 and c>=-1, kappa>=-4; G=-H attains -4. For the upper equality witness, H=1 has only its empty coefficient and G has mean 1/2, so A=1/4,c=1/2. This proves sharpness within actual Boolean pairs.

Additional physical interpretation: d=Pr(G!=H) satisfies c=1-2d, so

    kappa=2(1-2d-A) <= 4d(1-2d).

A positive leading tax requires d<1/2. If kappa>=1/2-epsilon, equation (4) gives |c-1/2|<=sqrt(epsilon/2), |d-1/4|<=sqrt(epsilon/8), and ||P_D a-c b||<=sqrt(epsilon/2). Thus near-maximal leading tax demands physical disagreement near 1/4 and a nearly proportional common-support projection.

At this point the fixed-pair mechanism alone leaves unsupported the exchange

    limsup_{p->0} sup_{dimension, residual biases, G,H}
        (J_p-M_p)/(p log2(1/p)) <= 1/2.

The fixed-pair expansions give no dimension-uniform O(p) remainder. Children and residual biases changing with p can move formerly positive b_T^2 toward zero at the same scale. The following independent two-dimensional uncertainty argument resolves the leading-coefficient exchange without relying on those remainders.

### Uniform singleton tax: the remaining leading-coefficient gap closes

For every finite actual Boolean sign under arbitrary interior product biases and a singleton physical block with bias p, put r=min(p,1-p), delta=1-sqrt(1-r). Then

    J_X-M_X <= tau(r):=h2(r)-h2(delta).             (5)

This holds uniformly in the residual dimension, residual biases and the Boolean function. The displayed tax is positive at r=1/2 and is not claimed sharp there. One may use min{Delta(r),tau(r)}, where Delta(r)=h2(r)+log2(1-r) is B09's existing tax, if its pending theorem is accepted. Our independent result (5) alone is enough for the rare endpoint.

Proof of the needed two-dimensional inequality: let v be any unit real or complex vector in C^2 and U=[[sqrt(1-r),-sqrt(r)],[sqrt(r),sqrt(1-r)]]. Define u=min(|v_0|^2,|v_1|^2) and w=min(|(Uv)_0|^2,|(Uv)_1|^2). Choose the basis vector e which gives the larger entry of v, and the row-basis vector k which gives the larger entry of Uv. Their overlap has magnitude either sqrt(r) or sqrt(1-r), hence at most sqrt(1-r). The sum of their rank-one orthogonal projectors has largest eigenvalue 1+|<e,k>| (diagonalize the span of e,k). Thus

    (1-u)+(1-w) <= 1+sqrt(1-r),
    u+w >= delta.                                 (6)

Here u,w are in [0,1/2] and delta is in [0,1-1/sqrt(2)] subset [0,1/2]. If either u or w is at least delta, monotonicity of h2 on [0,1/2] gives h2(u)+h2(w)>=h2(delta). Otherwise w>=delta-u, so monotonicity and the concavity of h2 give

    h2(u)+h2(w) >= h2(u)+h2(delta-u) >= h2(delta).

The final inequality follows because the concave function h2(s)+h2(delta-s), s in [0,delta], attains its minimum at an endpoint. Therefore

    H(|v|^2)+H(|Uv|^2) >= h2(delta).               (7)

For the actual Boolean function, normalize each residual spectral fiber of the parent by its marginal q_T. The spectral vector (coefficients without/with X) and the posterior physical vector (sqrt(1-p)hat H(T),sqrt(p)hat G(T)) are related by U, up to row/column signs or swaps. Each squared fiber entropy is respectively H(S_X|T) and H(X|T). Averaging (7) yields

    M_X+H(X|T)>=h2(delta),
    J_X=H(X)-H(X|T)<=h2(r)-h2(delta)-M_X.

This proves (5), with zero fibers omitted. It reconstructs the singleton transform and does not use the pending B09 uncertainty theorem. At exactly r=1/2 the stronger direct fact H(|v|^2)+H(|Uv|^2)>=1 is the usual mutually unbiased two-dimensional entropy inequality; we do not independently reprove that sharper endpoint in this run and do not need it for (5).

Since delta=r/2+O(r^2), elementary binary-entropy expansion gives

    tau(r) = (r/2)log2(1/r) + ((log2(e)-1)/2)r
             + O(r^2 log(1/r)).                    (8)

The scalar upper tax in (5) is uniform. The actual H=1, E G=1/2 witness from the fixed-pair theorem gives the matching lower coefficient. Consequently

    lim_{p->0} sup_{all finite actual Boolean signs,
                  all interior residual product biases}
       (J_X-M_X)/(p log2(1/p)) = 1/2.              (9)

The upper bound follows from (5)-(8) before taking the supremum, and the lower bound from one fixed actual Boolean pair. This closes the leading-coefficient exchange that the earlier fixed-pair mechanism could not justify. The first remaining unsupported line is a nonreusing physical influence/composition payment of M_X plus this tax, or an exact all-p optimal tax; neither is proved here.

# A096 stage 2: universal payment of the level-one-to-two erasure

## exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

Following B20 STREAM_NOTES.md v2 (2026-10-11 23:39Z), this stage retires the singleton-tax topic and pays the next level of the C018 erasure functional. For every finite n>=0 and every Boolean sign f on the uniform cube, write a_S=hat f(S), epsilon_i=Inf_i(f), I=sum_i epsilon_i, and phi(x,y)=(x+y)h2(y/(x+y)), with phi(x,0)=phi(0,y)=0. All entropies are in bits. Define

    W2=sum_{i<j}a_{ij}^2,
    H2=sum_{i<j}a_{ij}^2 log2(1/a_{ij}^2),
    T12=sum_i sum_{j!=i}phi(a_i^2,a_{ij}^2).

The theorem proved here, uniformly in dimension and without any structural restriction on f, is

    H2 <= (155600/50301) I,
    T12 <= (16700/2187) I < 8 I   when I>0.

For constants I=H2=T12=0. The constants are explicit upper bounds, not claimed sharp. The proof uses the full Boolean constraint through its equivalent pointwise form f^2=1 and the resulting {0,+1,-1}-valued derivatives. A norm-one real Fourier vector alone is insufficient. This closes the precise level-one-to-two target issued to A096. It does not bound all higher erasure levels or solve full FEI.

## sources actually read, revisions and theorem locators
